- White has played Ng1-f3 and then Nf3xe5, winning my e5 pawn. The knight now sits on e5, attacking f7, d7, and c6.
- I'm currently down a pawn, but that knight on e5 is undefended. If I can attack it with a pawn, it must retreat, gaining me a tempo.

**Key variation check:**
- After **d7-d6**, my pawn attacks the e5 knight, forcing it to move:
  - 2.Nxf7?? Kxf7 — I win the piece (nothing defends f7)
  - 2.Nd7?? Qxd7 — I win the piece
  - 2.Nc6? bxc6 — I win the piece
  - So White must retreat with 2.Nf3 (or 2.Nc4/Ng4), giving me a free tempo.
- Bonus: d6 also opens the c8-bishop's diagonal (d7-e6-f5-g4) toward White's kingside, so it's a developing move too.
- Alternatives like Nf6?? actually lose material to 2.Nxf6+ gxf6 (trade on top of being a pawn down), and Qh4 attacks nothing since f2 is defended by the king.
